about="#mwt30"> </span><span class="ext-phonos" typeof="mw:Extension/phonos" about="#mwt30" data-mw='{"name":"phonos","attrs":{"file":"Axolotl.ogg"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'><span data-nosnippet="" role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/58/Axolotl.ogg/Axolotl.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" title="Play audio" oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt navigation-not-searchable"><a href="/wiki/File:Axolotl.ogg" title="File:Axolotl.ogg">ⓘ</a></sup></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_with_Nahuatl_languages_IPA" about="#mwt30"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_including_recorded_pronunciations" about="#mwt30" id="mwHQ"/>; <i id="mwHg"><b id="mwHw">Ambystoma mexicanum</b></i>) is a species of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mole_salamander" title="Mole salamander" id="mwIA">mole salamander</a>. It is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neotenic" title="Neotenic" class="mw-redirect" id="mwIQ">neotenic</a>, reaching <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_maturity" title="Sexual maturity" id="mwIg">sexual maturity</a> without undergoing <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metamorphosis" title="Metamorphosis" id="mwIw">metamorphosis</a>, and the adults remain <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquatic_animal" title="Aquatic animal" id="mwJA">fully aquatic</a> with obvious <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/External_gills" title="External gills" id="mwJQ">external gills</a>. Axolotls may be difficult to distinguish from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadpole" title="Tadpole" id="mwJg">larval stage</a> of other neotenic adult mole salamanders, in particular the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiger_salamander" title="Tiger salamander" id="mwJw">tiger salamander</a>, or other species such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mudpuppies" title="Mudpuppies" class="mw-redirect" id="mwKA">mudpuppies</a>.<sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:3_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:3-4" id="mwKQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwKg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKw">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwLQ">Axolotls originally inhabited a system of interconnected <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wetlands" title="Wetlands" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLg">wetlands</a> and lakes in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highland" title="Highland" id="mwLw">highlands</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico" id="mwMA">Mexico</a>. They were known to inhabit the smaller lakes of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Xochimilco" title="Lake Xochimilco" id="mwMQ">Xochimilco</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Chalco" title="Lake Chalco" id="mwMg">Chalco</a> and are presumed to have inhabited the larger lakes of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Texcoco" title="Lake Texcoco" id="mwMw">Texcoco</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Zumpango" title="Lake Zumpango" id="mwNA">Zumpango</a>. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desiccation" title="Desiccation" id="mwNQ">desiccation</a> of these lakes, initiated by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztecs" title="Aztecs" id="mwNg">Aztecs</a> and accelerated during the 20th century,<sup about="#mwt35" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwNw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOQ">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt38" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwOw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwPw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwQA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQg">]</span></span></a></sup> has led to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Habitat_destruction" title="Habitat destruction" id="mwQw">destruction</a> of much of the axolotl's natural habitat, an area now largely occupied by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City" title="Mexico City" id="mwRA">Mexico City</a>. Wild axolotls have been driven to near <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extinction" title="Extinction" id="mwRQ">extinction</a> by the introduction of invasive species such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tilapia" title="Tilapia" id="mwRg">tilapia</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carp" title="Carp" id="mwRw">carp</a>; with a decreasing <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_(biology)" title="Population (biology)" id="mwSA">population</a> of around 50 to 1,000 adult individuals, the species has been assessed as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critically_endangered" title="Critically endangered" id="mwSQ">critically endangered</a> by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Union_for_Conservation_of_Nature" title="International Union for Conservation of Nature" id="mwSg">International Union for Conservation of Nature</a> (IUCN) and is listed under <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appendix_II" title="Appendix II" class="mw-redirect" id="mwSw">Appendix II</a> of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species (CITES).<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-CITES_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"CITES"}}'><a href="#cite_note-CITES-2" id="mwTA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTg">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwUA">There currently exists a large <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captivity_(animal)" title="Captivity (animal)" id="mwUQ">captive</a> population of axolotls, with specimens used extensively in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_testing" title="Animal testing" id="mwUg">scientific research</a> because of their unusual ability to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regeneration_(biology)" title="Regeneration (biology)" id="mwUw">regenerate</a> body parts, including limbs, gills and parts of their eyes and brains. The species is also used as a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Model_organism" title="Model organism" id="mwVA">model organism</a>. As <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquarium" title="Aquarium" id="mwVQ">aquarium</a> technology has developed, axolotls have become a common exhibit in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoos" title="Zoos" class="mw-redirect" id="mwVg">zoos</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_aquariums" title="Public aquariums" class="mw-redirect" id="mwVw">public aquariums</a>, as well as an occasional pet in home aquariums. The axolotl is a popular subject in contemporary culture, inspiring a number of works and characters in the media.</p>

You can help by <a rel="mw:ExtLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Axolotl&amp;action=edit&amp;section=" class="external text" data-mw-original-href="//en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Axolotl&amp;action=edit&amp;section=">adding missing information</a>.  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2025</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt43"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_to_be_expanded_from_June_2025"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:All_articles_to_be_expanded"/></span>
<p id="mwXQ">The term "axolotl" is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahuatl" title="Nahuatl" id="mwXg">Nahuatl</a> word which has been translated variably as "water slave", "water servant", "water sprite", "water player", "water monstrosity", "water twin", or "water dog".<sup about="#mwt48" class="mw-ref reference" id="

					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr" data-mw-parsoid-version="0.24.0.0-alpha23" data-mw-html-version="2.8.0"><section data-mw-section-id="0" id="mwAQ"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none" about="#mwt1" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"short description","href":"./Template:Short_description"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Order of amphibians"}},"i":0}}]}'>Order of amphibians</div><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt1"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_short_description"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Short_description_matches_Wikidata"/></span>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt2" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"for-multi","href":"./Template:For-multi"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"the mythical creature"},"2":{"wt":"Salamanders in folklore"},"3":{"wt":"other uses"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1368532237" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt3" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+.mw-empty-elt+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA">For the mythical creature, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salamanders_in_folklore" title="Salamanders in folklore">Salamanders in folklore</a>. For other uses, see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salamander_(disambiguation)" title="Salamander (disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig">Salamander (disambiguation)</a>.</div>

Click here for more information.]]\n"}}'/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Good_articles"/>

</p><table class="infobox biota" style="text-align: left; width: 200px; font-size: 100%" about="#mwt7">

</tbody></table><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt7"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1289143401" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt13" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Taxobox/core/styles.css"}}'>@media(max-width:640px){body:not(.skin-minerva) .mw-parser-output .infobox{width:100%!important}body:not(.skin-minerva) .mw-parser-output .infobox th{width:50%}}@media screen{html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .infobox.biota tr{background:transparent!important}html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .infobox.biota img{background:transparent}}@media screen and (prefers-color-scheme:dark){html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .infobox.biota tr{background:transparent!important}html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .infobox.biota img{background:white}}.mw-parser-output .infobox.biota .taxobox-edit-taxonomy img{background:transparent!important}body.skin-vector .mw-parser-output table.biota.infobox{margin-top:0.5em}body.skin--responsive .mw-parser-output table.biota.infobox tr.taxonrow2 td{padding:2px 10px}</style><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_'species'_microformats"/></span>

<p id="mwCw"><b id="mwDA">Salamanders</b> are a group of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibian" title="Amphibian" id="mwDQ">amphibians</a> typically characterized by their <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lizard" title="Lizard" id="mwDg">lizard</a>-like appearance, with slender bodies, blunt snouts, short limbs projecting at right angles to the body, and the presence of a tail in both larvae and adults. All ten extant salamander families are grouped together under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_(biology)" title="Order (biology)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDw">order</a> <b id="mwEA">Urodela</b>, the sole surviving order from the group <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caudata" title="Caudata" id="mwEQ">Caudata</a>.<sup about="#mwt19" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-AOTW_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"AOTW"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-AOTW-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-AOTW-2" id="mwEg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwEw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFA">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwFQ">]</span></span></a></sup> <i id="mwFg">Urodela</i> is a scientific Latin term based on the Ancient Greek <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text" about="#mwt14" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwFw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"grc"},"2":{"wt":"οὐρά"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="grc">οὐρά</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Ancient_Greek_(to_1453)-language_text" about="#mwt14" id="mwGA"/> <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text" about="#mwt15" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwGQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"grc"},"2":{"wt":"δήλη"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="grc">δήλη</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Ancient_Greek_(to_1453)-language_text" about="#mwt15" id="mwGg"/> (<i id="mwGw">ourà dēlē</i>), "conspicuous tail". <i id="mwHA">Caudata</i> is the Latin for "tailed ones", from <span title="Latin-language text" about="#mwt16" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"la"},"2":{"wt":"cauda"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="la">cauda</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Latin-language_text" about="#mwt16" id="mwHg"/>, "tail". </p>

<p id="mwHw">Salamander diversity is highest in eastern <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_America" title="North America" id="mwIA">North America</a>, especially in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Appalachian_Mountains" title="Appalachian Mountains" id="mwIQ">Appalachian Mountains</a>; most <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Species" title="Species" id="mwIg">species</a> are found in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holarctic_realm" title="Holarctic realm" id="mwIw">Holarctic realm</a>, with some species present in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neotropical_realm" title="Neotropical realm" id="mwJA">Neotropical realm</a>. Salamanders never have more than four toes on their front legs and five on their rear legs,<sup about="#mwt20" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-3" id="mwJQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwJg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwKA">]</span></span></a></sup> but some species have fewer digits and others lack hind limbs. Their permeable skin usually makes them reliant on habitats in or near water or other cool, damp places. Some salamander species are fully aquatic throughout their lives, some take to the water intermittently, and others are entirely terrestrial as adults.</p>

<p id="mwKQ">This group of amphibians is capable of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regeneration_(biology)" title="Regeneration (biology)" id="mwKg">regenerating</a> lost limbs as well as other damaged parts of their bodies. Researchers seek to reverse engineer the regenerative processes for potential human medical applications, such as brain and spinal cord injury treatment or preventing harmful scarring during heart surgery recovery.<sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwKw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLQ">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLg">]</span></span></a></sup> The salamander's ability to regenerate is not limited to limbs but extends to vital organs such as the heart, jaw, and parts of the spinal cord, contrasting most other <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertebrate" title="Vertebrate" id="mwLw">vertebrates</a>. ⁤⁤This ability is special in that it occurs without any type of scarring. ⁤⁤This has made salamanders a widespread <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Model_organism" title="Model organism" id="mwMA">model organism</a> in scientific research aimed at understanding and achieving regenerative processes for medical advancements in human and animal biology.<sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwMQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMw">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwNQ">Members of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_(taxonomy)" title="Family (taxonomy)" id="mwNg">family</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salamandridae" title="Salamandridae" id="mwNw">Salamandridae</a> are mostly known as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newt" title="Newt" id="mwOA">newts</a> and lack the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costal_groove" title="Costal groove" class="mw-redirect" id="mwOQ">costal grooves</a> along the sides of their bodies typical of other groups. The skin of some species contains the powerful poison <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetrodotoxin" title="Tetrodotoxin" id="mwOg">tetrodotoxin</a>; these salamanders tend to be slow-moving and have bright <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warning_coloration" title="Warning coloration" class="mw-redirect" id="mwOw">warning coloration</a> to advertise their toxicity. Salamanders typically lay eggs in water and have aquatic larvae, but great variation occurs in their <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biological_life_cycle" title="Biological life cycle" id="mwPA">lifecycles</a>. Some species in harsh environments reproduce while still in the Salamander larval state.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwPQ" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwPg" aria-labelledby="Etymology"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Etymology">Etymology</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Salamander&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwPw">The word <i id="mwQA">salamander</i> comes from Old French <i id="mwQQ">salamandre</i> from Latin <i id="mwQg">salamandra</i> from Greek <span title="Greek-language text" about="#mwt27" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwQw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"el"},"2":{"wt":"σαλαμάνδρα"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="el">σαλαμάνδρα</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Greek-language_text" about="#mwt27" id="mwRA"/> (<i id="mwRQ">salamándra</i>), used specifically for the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_salamander" title="Fire salamander" id="mwRg">fire salamander</a>;<sup about="#mwt30" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwRw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSg">]</span></span></a></sup> it is of uncertain origin, possibly pre-Greek.<sup about="#mwt33" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-7" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-7" id="mwSw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwTw" aria-labelledby="Description"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Description">Description</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Salamander&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Description"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwUA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwUQ"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg/250px-Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg/500px-Salamandra_lanzai13.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="770" data-file-height="481" data-file-type="bitmap" height="156" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwUg"/></a><figcaption id="mwUw">X-ray image of salamander</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwVA">The skin lacks scales and is moist and smooth to the touch, except in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newt" title="Newt" id="mwVQ">newts</a> of the Salamandridae, which may have velvety or warty skin, wet to the touch. The skin may be drab or brightly colored, exhibiting various patterns of stripes, bars, spots, blotches, or dots. Male newts become dramatically colored during the breeding season. Cave species dwelling in darkness lack pigmentation and have a translucent pink or pearlescent appearance.<sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-EoR_pp.60–68_8-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"EoR pp.60–68"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-EoR_pp.60–68-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-EoR_pp.60–68-8" id="mwVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwWg">Salamanders range in size from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minute_salamanders" title="Minute salamanders" class="mw-redirect" id="mwWw">minute salamanders</a>, with a total length of <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1154941027" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt37" id="mwXA" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Fraction/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"27"},"2":{"wt":"mm"},"3":{"wt":"in"},"frac":{"wt":"8"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .frac{white-space:nowrap}.mw-parser-output .frac .num,.mw-parser-output .frac .den{font-size:80%;line-height:0;vertical-align:super}.mw-parser-output .frac .den{vertical-align:sub}.mw-parser-output .sr-only{border:0;clip:rect(0,0,0,0);clip-path:polygon(0px 0px,0px 0px,0px 0px);height:1px;margin:-1px;overflow:hidden;padding:0;position:absolute;width:1px}</style><span about="#mwt37">27</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt37"> </span><span about="#mwt37">mm (</span><span class="frac" about="#mwt37">1<span class="sr-only">+</span><span class="num">1</span><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁄</span><span class="den">8</span></span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt37"> </span><span about="#mwt37">in)</span>, including the tail, to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_giant_salamander" title="Chinese giant salamander" id="mwXQ">Chinese giant salamander</a> which reaches <span about="#mwt39" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwXg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"1.8"},"2":{"wt":"m"},"3":{"wt":"ft"},"4":{"wt":"0"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>1.8</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt39"> </span><span about="#mwt39">m (6</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt39"> </span><span about="#mwt39">ft)</span> and weighs up to <span about="#mwt40" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwXw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"65"},"2":{"wt":"kg"},"3":{"wt":"lb"},"round":{"wt":"5"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>65</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt40"> </span><span about="#mwt40">kg (145</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt40"> </span><span about="#mwt40">lb)</span>. All the largest species are found in the four families <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_salamander" title="Giant salamander" id="mwYA">giant salamanders</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirenidae" title="Sirenidae" id="mwYQ">sirens</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphiuma" title="Amphiuma" id="mwYg">Congo eels</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proteidae" title="Proteidae" id="mwYw">Proteidae</a>, which are all aquatic and obligate <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoteny" title="Neoteny" id="mwZA">paedomorphs</a>.<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwZQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwZg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZw">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-10" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-10" id="mwaQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwag"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaw">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbA">]</span></span></a></sup> Some of the largest terrestrial salamanders, which go through full metamorphosis, belong to the family of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_giant_salamander" title="Pacific giant salamander" id="mwbQ">Pacific giant salamanders</a>, and are much smaller.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwbg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcA">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Most salamanders are between <span about="#mwt41" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwcg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"10"},"2":{"wt":"and"},"3":{"wt":"20"},"4":{"wt":"cm"},"5":{"wt":"in"},"6":{"wt":"0"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>10 and 20</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt41"> </span><span about="#mwt41">cm (4 and 8</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt41"> </span><span about="#mwt41">in)</span> in length.<sup about="#mwt45" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins3_12-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins3"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-stebbins3-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins3-12" id="mwcw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwdw" aria-labelledby="Trunk,_limbs_and_tail"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Trunk,_limbs_and_tail"><span id="Trunk.2C_limbs_and_tail" typeof="mw:FallbackId"></span>Trunk, limbs and tail</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Salamander&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Trunk, limbs and tail"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mweA">An adult salamander generally resembles a small lizard, having a basal <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetrapod" title="Tetrapod" id="mweQ">tetrapod</a> body form with a cylindrical trunk, four limbs, and a long tail. Except in the family Salamandridae, the head, body, and tail have a number of vertical depressions in the surface which run from the mid-dorsal region to the ventral area and are known as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costal_groove" title="Costal groove" class="mw-redirect" id="mweg">costal grooves</a>. Their function seems to be to help keep the skin moist by channeling water over the surface of the body.<sup about="#mwt49" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwew"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfQ">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwfw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:S_lacertina_USGS.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwgA"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:S_lacertina_USGS.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/S_lacertina_USGS.jpg/250px-S_lacertina_USGS.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4d/S_lacertina_USGS.jpg/500px-S_lacertina_USGS.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="540" data-file-type="bitmap" height="188" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwgQ"/></a><figcaption id="mwgg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirenidae" title="Sirenidae" id="mwgw">Sirens</a> have an eel-like appearance.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwhA">Some aquatic species, such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sirenidae" title="Sirenidae" id="mwhQ">sirens</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphiuma" title="Amphiuma" id="mwhg">amphiumas</a>, have reduced or absent hind limbs, giving them an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eel" title="Eel" id="mwhw">eel</a>-like appearance, but in most species, the front and rear limbs are about the same length and project sideward, barely raising the trunk off the ground. The feet are broad with short digits, usually four on the front feet and five on the rear. Salamanders do not have claws, and the shape of the foot varies according to the animal's habitat. Climbing species have elongated, square-tipped toes, while rock-dwellers have larger feet with short, blunt toes. The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolitoglossa" title="Bolitoglossa" id="mwiA">tree-climbing salamander</a> (<i id="mwiQ">Bolitoglossa</i> sp.) has plate-like webbed feet which adhere to smooth surfaces by suction, while the rock-climbing <i id="mwig"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydromantes" title="Hydromantes" id="mwiw">Hydromantes</a></i> species from California have feet with fleshy webs and short digits and use their tails as an extra limb. When ascending, the tail props up the rear of the body, while one hind foot moves forward and then swings to the other side to provide support as the other hind foot advances.<sup about="#mwt50" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins30_14-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins30"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-stebbins30-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins30-14" id="mwjA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjg">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwjw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwkA">In larvae and aquatic salamanders, the tail is laterally flattened, has dorsal and ventral fins, and undulates from side to side to propel the animal through the water. In the families <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambystomatidae" title="Ambystomatidae" id="mwkQ">Ambystomatidae</a> and Salamandridae, the male's tail, which is larger than that of the female, is used during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amplexus" title="Amplexus" id="mwkg">amplexus embrace</a> to propel the mating couple to a secluded location. In terrestrial species, the tail moves to counterbalance the animal as it runs, while in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arboreal_salamander" title="Arboreal salamander" id="mwkw">arboreal salamander</a> and other tree-climbing species, it is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehensile_tail" title="Prehensile tail" id="mwlA">prehensile</a>. The tail is also used by certain <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lungless_salamander" title="Lungless salamander" class="mw-redirect" id="mwlQ">plethodontid salamanders</a> that can jump, to help launch themselves into the air.<sup about="#mwt51" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins30_14-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins30-14" id="mwlg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwlw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmA">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmQ">]</span></span></a></sup> The tail is used in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Courtship_display" title="Courtship display" id="mwmg">courtship</a> and as a storage organ for proteins and lipids. It also functions as a defense against predation, when it may be lashed at the attacker or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autotomy" title="Autotomy" id="mwmw">autotomised</a> when grabbed. Unlike frogs, an adult salamander is able to regenerate limbs and its tail when these are lost.<sup about="#mwt52" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins30_14-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins30-14" id="mwnA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwng">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwoA" aria-labelledby="Skin"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Skin">Skin</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Salamander&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Skin"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwoQ"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taricha_granulosa_(Rough-skinned_newt).JPG" class="mw-file-description" id="mwog"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Taricha_granulosa_(Rough-skinned_newt).JPG" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Taricha_granulosa_%28Rough-skinned_newt%29.JPG/250px-Taricha_granulosa_%28Rough-skinned_newt%29.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Taricha_granulosa_%28Rough-skinned_newt%29.JPG/500px-Taricha_granulosa_%28Rough-skinned_newt%29.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="1591" data-file-height="1878" data-file-type="bitmap" height="224" width="190" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" id="mwow"/></a><figcaption id="mwpA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rough-skinned_newt" title="Rough-skinned newt" id="mwpQ">Rough-skinned newt</a></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwpg">The skin of salamanders, in common with other amphibians, is thin, permeable to water, serves as a respiratory membrane, and is well-supplied with glands. It has highly <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornified" title="Cornified" class="mw-redirect" id="mwpw">cornified</a> outer layers, renewed periodically through a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moulting" title="Moulting" id="mwqA">skin shedding</a> process controlled by hormones from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pituitary" title="Pituitary" class="mw-redirect" id="mwqQ">pituitary</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thyroid" title="Thyroid" id="mwqg">thyroid</a> glands. During moulting, the skin initially breaks around the mouth, and the animal moves forward through the gap to shed the skin. When the front limbs have been worked clear, a series of body ripples pushes the skin toward the rear. The hind limbs are extracted and push the skin farther back, before it is eventually freed by friction as the salamander moves forward with the tail pressed against the ground.<sup about="#mwt53" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins16_15-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins16"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-stebbins16-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins16-15" id="mwqw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrQ">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwrg">]</span></span></a></sup> The animal often then eats the resulting sloughed skin.<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-EoR_pp.60–68_8-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"EoR pp.60–68"}}'><a href="#cite_note-EoR_pp.60–68-8" id="mwrw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwsw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mucous_gland" title="Mucous gland" id="mwtA">Glands</a> in the skin discharge <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mucus" title="Mucus" id="mwtQ">mucus</a> which keeps the skin moist, an important factor in skin respiration and thermoregulation. The sticky layer helps protect against bacterial infections and molds, reduces friction when swimming, and makes the animal slippery and more difficult for predators to catch. Granular glands scattered on the upper surface, particularly the head, back, and tail, produce repellent or toxic secretions.<sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-stebbins16_15-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"stebbins16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-stebbins16-15" id="mwtg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwtw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuA">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuQ">]</span></span></a></sup> Some salamander toxins are particularly potent. For example, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rough-skinned_newt" title="Rough-skinned newt" id="mwug">rough-skinned newt</a> (<i id="mwuw">Taricha granulosa</i>) produces the neurotoxin <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tetrodotoxin" title="Tetrodotoxin" id="mwvA">tetrodotoxin</a>, the most toxic nonprotein substance known. Handling the newts does no harm, but ingestion of even a minute fragment of skin is deadly. In feeding trials, fish, frogs, reptiles, birds, and mammals were all found to be susceptible.<sup about="#mwt58" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwvQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwvg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvw">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwwA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwwQ">Mature adults of some salamander species have "nuptial" glandular tissue in their <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloaca" title="Cloaca" id="mwwg">cloacae</a>, at the base of their tails, on their heads or under their chins. Some females release <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pheromone" title="Pheromone" id="mwww">chemical substances</a>, possibly from the ventral cloacal gland, to attract males, but males do not seem to use pheromones for this purpose.<sup about="#mwt62" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Kentwood_17-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Kentwood"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Kentwood-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Kentwood-17" id="mwxA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxw">]</span></span></a></sup> In some <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plethodontidae" title="Plethodontidae" id="mwyA">plethodonts</a>, males have conspicuous mental glands on the chin which are pressed against the females' nostrils during the courtship ritual. They may function to speed up the mating process, reducing the risk of its being disrupted by a predator or rival male.<sup about="#mwt65" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwyQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwyw">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzA">]</span></span></a></sup> The gland at the base of the tail in <i id="mwzQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plethodon_cinereus" title="Plethodon cinereus" class="mw-redirect" id="mwzg">Plethodon cinereus</a></i> is used to mark <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fecal_pellets" title="Fecal pellets" class="mw-redirect" id="mwzw">fecal pellets</a> to proclaim territorial ownership.<sup about="#mwt66" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Kentwood_17-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Kentwood"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Kentwood-17" id="mw0A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw0Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0g">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0w">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mw1A" aria-labelledby="Senses"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Senses">Senses</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Salamander&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Senses"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mw1Q"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P_anguinus-head1.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mw1g"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:P_anguinus-head1.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/P_anguinus-head1.jpg/250px-P_anguinus-head1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/5a/P_anguinus-head1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="206" data-file-type="bitmap" height="172" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mw1w"/></a><figcaption id="mw2A">The front part of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olm" title="Olm" id="mw2Q">olm</a>'s head carries sensitive chemo-, mechano-, and electroreceptors.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mw2g"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biofluorescent_patterns_across_salamander_diversity_and_anatomy_-_41598_2020_59528_Fig1.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mw2w"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biofluorescent_patterns_across_salamander_diversity_and_anatomy_-_41598_2020_59528_Fig1.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Biofluorescent_patterns_across_salamander_diversity_and_anatomy_-_41598_2020_59528_Fig1.jpg/250px-Biofluorescent_patterns_across_salamander_diversi

about="#mwt17" typeof="mw:Transclusion" class="mw-redirect" id="mwDQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"langx","href":"./Template:Langx"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"nci"},"2":{"wt":"Xōchimīlco"}},"i":0}}]}'>Classical Nahuatl</a><span about="#mwt17">: </span><i lang="nci" about="#mwt17">Xōchimīlco</i><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Classical_Nahuatl-language_text" about="#mwt17" id="mwDg"/> <span class="IPA nowrap" lang="nah-Latn-fonipa" about="#mwt18" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwDw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"IPA","href":"./Template:IPA"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"nah"},"2":{"wt":"ʃoːtʃiˈmiːlko"},"3":{"wt":""},"4":{"wt":"Xochimilko.ogg"}},"i":0}}]}'><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Nahuatl" title="Help:IPA/Nahuatl"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span>ʃoːtʃiˈmiːlko<span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></a></span><span about="#mwt18"> </span><span class="ext-phonos" typeof="mw:Extension/phonos" about="#mwt18" data-mw='{"name":"phonos","attrs":{"file":"Xochimilko.ogg"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'><span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-buttonElement-size-medium oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui='{"_":"mw.Phonos.PhonosButton","href":"\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/9\/9f\/Xochimilko.ogg\/Xochimilko.ogg.mp3","rel":["nofollow"],"framed":false,"icon":"volumeUp","data":{"ipa":"","text":"","lang":"en","wikibase":"","file":"Xochimilko.ogg"},"classes":["noexcerpt","ext-phonos-PhonosButton","ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel"]}'><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/9/9f/Xochimilko.ogg/Xochimilko.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" title="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt navigation-not-searchable"><a href="/wiki/File:Xochimilko.ogg" title="File:Xochimilko.ogg">ⓘ</a></sup></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_with_Nahuatl_languages_IPA" about="#mwt18"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_including_recorded_pronunciations" about="#mwt18" id="mwEA"/>) is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boroughs_of_Mexico_City" title="Boroughs of Mexico City" id="mwEQ">borough</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language" about="#mwt20" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwEg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"langx","href":"./Template:Langx"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"demarcación territorial"}},"i":0}}]}'>Spanish</a><span about="#mwt20">: </span><i lang="es" about="#mwt20">demarcación territorial</i><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt20" id="mwEw"/>) of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico_City" title="Mexico City" id="mwFA">Mexico City</a>. The borough is centered on the formerly independent city of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xochimilco_(altepetl)" title="Xochimilco (altepetl)" id="mwFQ">Xochimilco</a>, which was established on what was the southern shore of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Xochimilco" title="Lake Xochimilco" id="mwFg">Lake Xochimilco</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Columbian_era" title="Pre-Columbian era" id="mwFw">precolonial</a> period.</p>

<p id="mwGA">Today, the borough consists of the 18 <span title="Spanish-language text" about="#mwt21" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwGQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"barrios"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="es">barrios</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt21" id="mwGg"/>, or neighborhoods, of this city along with 14 <span title="Spanish-language text" about="#mwt22" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwGw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"pueblos"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="es">pueblos</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt22" id="mwHA"/>, or villages, that surround it, covering an area of <span about="#mwt23" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"125"},"2":{"wt":"km2"},"3":{"wt":"sqmi"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>125</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt23"> </span><span about="#mwt23">km</span><sup about="#mwt23">2</sup><span about="#mwt23"> (48</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt23"> </span><span about="#mwt23">sq</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt23"> </span><span about="#mwt23">mi)</span>. The borough is in the southeastern part of the city and has an identity that is separate from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_center_of_Mexico_City" title="Historic center of Mexico City" id="mwHg">historic center of Mexico City</a>, due to its historic separation from that city during most of its history.</p>

<p id="mwHw">Xochimilco is best known for its canals, which are left from what was an extensive lake and canal system that connected most of the settlements of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_of_Mexico" title="Valley of Mexico" id="mwIA">Valley of Mexico</a>. These canals, along with artificial islands called <i id="mwIQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinampa" title="Chinampa" id="mwIg">chinampas</a></i>, attract tourists and other city residents to ride on colorful <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gondola" title="Gondola" id="mwIw">gondola</a>-like boats called <span title="Spanish-language text" about="#mwt24" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwJA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"[[trajinera]]s"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="es"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trajinera" title="Trajinera">trajineras</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt24" id="mwJQ"/> around the <span about="#mwt25" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwJg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"170"},"2":{"wt":"km"},"3":{"wt":"mi"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>170</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt25"> </span><span about="#mwt25">km (110</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt25"> </span><span about="#mwt25">mi)</span> of canals. This canal and chinampa system, as a vestige of the area's precolonial past, has made Xochimilco a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site" id="mwJw">World Heritage Site</a>.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwKA" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwKQ" aria-labelledby="The_city_and_borough"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="The_city_and_borough">The city and borough</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Xochimilco&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: The city and borough"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwKg"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG" class="mw-file-description" id="mwKw"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG/250px-SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG/500px-SanBernadinoPlazaXochi.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" data-file-type="bitmap" height="167" width="250" class="mw-file-element" id="mwLA"/></a><figcaption id="mwLQ">View of part of the main plaza of the center of Xochimilco</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwLg">The borough of Xochimilco was created in 1928, when the federal government reorganized the Federal District of Mexico City into <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boroughs_of_Mexico_City" title="Boroughs of Mexico City" id="mwLw">sixteen boroughs</a>. The Xochimilco borough was centered on what was the city of Xochimilco, which had been an independent settlement from the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-Hispanic_period" title="Pre-Hispanic period" class="mw-redirect" id="mwMA">pre-Hispanic period</a> to the 20th century.<sup about="#mwt27" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Breve_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Breve"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Breve-2" id="mwMQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt30" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-hisdel_3-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"hisdel"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-hisdel-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-hisdel-3" id="mwNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">]</span></span></a></sup> The area's historic separation from Mexico City proper remains in its culture. While officially part of the city, its identity is more like a suburb.<sup about="#mwt33" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-yearlong_4-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"yearlong"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-yearlong-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-yearlong-4" id="mwOQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOw">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPA">]</span></span></a></sup> This historic center was designated as a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barrios_Mágicos_of_Mexico_City" title="Barrios Mágicos of Mexico City" class="mw-redirect" id="mwPQ">"Barrio Mágico"</a> by the city in 2011.<sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-mexdes2_5-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"mexdes2"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-mexdes2-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-mexdes2-5" id="mwPg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQQ">]</span></span></a></sup>  The borough is center-south of the historic center of Mexico City, and bordered by the boroughs of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlalpan" title="Tlalpan" id="mwQg">Tlalpan</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coyoacán" title="Coyoacán" id="mwQw">Coyoacán</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tláhuac" title="Tláhuac" id="mwRA">Tláhuac</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milpa_Alta" title="Milpa Alta" id="mwRQ">Milpa Alta</a>. It extends over <span about="#mwt26" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwRg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"125"},"2":{"wt":"km2"},"3":{"wt":"sqmi"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>125</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt26"> </span><span about="#mwt26">km</span><sup about="#mwt26">2</sup><span about="#mwt26"> (48</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt26"> </span><span about="#mwt26">sq</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt26"> </span><span about="#mwt26">mi)</span>, accounting for 8.4% of the Federal District's territory. It is the third largest borough, after Tlalpan, and Milpa Alta.<sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-magiasur_6-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"magiasur"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-magiasur-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-magiasur-6" id="mwRw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-desarrollo_7-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"desarrollo"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-desarrollo-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-desarrollo-7" id="mwSw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTg">]</span></span></a></sup> The borough has an emblem, also known as an <i id="mwTw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aztec" title="Aztec" class="mw-redirect" id="mwUA">Aztec</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glyph" title="Glyph" id="mwUQ">glyph</a></i>, which is a representation of the area's spongy soil from which two flowering plants emerge.<sup about="#mwt45" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ciudadmex_8-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ciudadmex"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-ciudadmex-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ciudadmex-8" id="mwUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVA">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVQ">]</span></span></a></sup> In spite of the serious environmental issues, 77.9% of the territory is designated as ecological reserve, 15.2% as residential and 4.6 as commercial and industrial.<sup about="#mwt46" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-desarrollo_7-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"desarrollo"}}'><a href="#cite_note-desarrollo-7" id="mwVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwWg">The borough is divided into eighteen "barrios," which make up the old city of Xochimilco and fourteen communities outside the traditional city called "pueblos." The barrios are El Rosario, Santa Crucita, Caltongo, San Lorenzo, San Diego, La Asunción, San Juan, San Antonio, Belem, San Cristóbal, San Esteban, La Santísima, La Guadalupita, La Concepción Tlacoapa, San Marcos and Xaltocan, The fourteen pueblos are <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_María_Tepepan" title="Santa María Tepepan" id="mwWw">Santa María Tepepan</a>, Santiago Tepalcatlalpan, San Mateo Xalpa, San Lorenzo Atemoaya, Santa Cruz Xochitepec, San Lucas Xochimanca, San Francisco Tlalnepantla, Santa María Nativitas, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gregorio_Atlapulco" title="San Gregorio Atlapulco" id="mwXA">San Gregorio Atlapulco</a>, Santiago Tulyehualco, San Luis Tlaxialtemalco, San Andrés Ahuayucan, Santa Cecilia Tepetlapa and San Cruz Acalpixca. There are also 45 smaller divisions called "colonias" and twenty major apartment complexes.<sup about="#mwt49" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Breve_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Breve"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Breve-2" id="mwXQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYA">]</span></span></a></sup> The city acts as the local government for all the communities of the borough, whether part of the city or not. These offices are located on Calle Guadalupe I. Ramirez 4, in the El Rosario area.<sup about="#mwt50" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ciudadmex_8-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ciudadmex"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ciudadmex-8" id="mwYQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZA">]</span></span></a></sup> The borough has <span about="#mwt47" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwZQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"11.4"},"2":{"wt":"km"},"3":{"wt":"mi"},"abbr":{"wt":"on"}},"i":0}}]}'>11.4</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt47"> </span><span about="#mwt47">km (7.1</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" about="#mwt47"> </span><span about="#mwt47">mi)</span> of primary roadway and <span about="#mwt48" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwZg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"convert","href":"./Template:Convert"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"4284733"},"2":{"wt":"m2"},"3":{"wt":"acre"},"4":{"wt":"1"}},"i":0}}]}'>4,284,733 square metres (1,058.8 acres)</span> of paved surface.<sup about="#mwt51" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-desarrollo_7-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"desarrollo"}}'><a href="#cite_note-desarrollo-7" id="mwZw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwaA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwag">]</span></span></a></sup> Major thoroughfares include the Xochimilco-Tulyehualco road, Nuevo León, Periférico Sur, Avenida Guadalupe and Calzada México-Xochimilco.<sup about="#mwt52" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ciudadmex_8-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ciudadmex"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ciudadmex-8" id="mwaw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbQ">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbg">]</span></span></a></sup> However, many of the areas of the borough are still semi-rural, with communities that still retain many old traditions and economic activities. For example, San Antonio Molotlán is noted for textiles and its <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinelos" title="Chinelos" id="mwbw">Chinelos</a> dancers.<sup about="#mwt53" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rincones54_9-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rincones54"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-rincones54-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rincones54-9" id="mwcA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcg">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcw">]</span></span></a></sup> San Lorenzo Tlaltecpan is known for the production of milk and there are still a large number of stables in the area.<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rincones54_9-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rincones54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rincones54-9" id="mwdA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdg">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mweA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fotos_de_Sta._Cecilia_2008_225.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mweQ"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fotos_de_Sta._Cecilia_2008_225.jpg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Fotos_de_Sta._Cecilia_2008_225.jpg/250px-Fotos_de_Sta._Cecilia_2008_225.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/4f/Fotos_de_Sta._Cecilia_2008_225.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail_unscaled 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="640" data-file-type="bitmap" height="253" width="190" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" id="mweg"/></a><figcaption id="mwew">Church at Santa Cecilia Tepetlapa</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwfA">The most notable neighborhoods/communities include Xaltocan, Ejidos de Tepepan, La Noria, Las Cruces, Ejidos de Xochimilco and San Gregorio Atlapulco.<sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-ciudadmex_8-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"ciudadmex"}}'><a href="#cite_note-ciudadmex-8" id="mwfQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgA">]</span></span></a></sup> San Francisco Caltongo is one of the oldest neighborhoods of the borough.<sup about="#mwt56" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-rincones54_9-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"rincones54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-rincones54-9" id="mwgQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgw">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhA">]</span></span></a></sup> Xaltocán began as a ranch or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hacienda" title="Hacienda" id="mwhQ">hacienda</a> that belonged to the indigenous <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cacique" title="Cacique" id="mwhg">caciques</a>. It was later donated to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Bernardino_de_Siena_Church,_Xochimilco" title="San Bernardino de Siena Church, Xochimilco" id="mwhw">San Bernardino de Siena monastery</a>. After the monastery was secularized, it became hacienda land again, but over time, parts were sold and it became the current area of Xochimilco. The church for this community was built in 1751 as the hacienda chapel. Originally, it was dedicated to Jesus, then <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candlemas" title="Candlemas" id="mwiA">Candlemas</a> and finally the Virgin of the Sorrows (Virgen de los Dolores). It officially became a sanctuary in 1951, declared by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archbishop_of_Mexico" title="Archbishop of Mexico" class="mw-
